The third branch now says neither direction is established. `test_no_completeness_prose_claims_what_its_own_status_denies` checks phrases rather than meaning, deliberately: the prose asserting a root was reached is a closed set here, so a new spelling of it has to pass the test to enter the record |
| D-224 | **`edits` holds prior version ids, oldest first, never the item's own id**, and a prior version is not an expected item | accepted | The schema types the field and no measurement can settle its meaning: `T-222` scanned 610 credential-free posts and found no edited post, `x fields tweet` declares `edits` with no surface at any tier, and search — the only way to hunt one directly — is Tier 2 and excluded. The report's own instruction is that `T-227` treat it as absent-unless-represented and **never as a field to guess**, so the handling is decided now rather than improvised against live data later. Excluding the current id is what makes one sentence true without exception: *nothing named in `edits` was observed*. X's `edit_history_tweet_ids` does include the current version, so an adapter must drop it — include it and that sentence needs a carve-out for the one id that was observed, which is the kind of qualifier a reader stops applying. Coverage is deliberately untouched: a prior version is the same post in a state that no longer exists, not another post in the conversation, so it is not an expected item, does not enter `expected_item_count`, and cannot turn a `PASS` into a `PARTIAL` — the honest statement about it is `edits` itself. Enforced by `edit_history_errors` over three schema-legal lies, since JSON Schema compares no two fields and the rejection catalogue cannot reach them |
| D-225 | `constants.OMISSION_REASONS` gains **`source_unavailable`**, the eleventh | accepted | An *included* post that was never observed — a tombstone — has no content to audit, and the omission is minted by `twitter.extract.create_pending_coverage` rather than chosen by an auditor. `other_explained` is the right value for a judgement call and the wrong one for a case the pipeline generates on every such run: "explain yourself" invites a different sentence each time for one structural fact. Additive, so no existing document becomes invalid, and `schemas/extraction_bundle.schema.json` was updated in the same step because `tests/test_index_schemas.py` asserts that enum **equals** the constant — a guard that caught the omission immediately |
| D-226 | What a **source** unit owes as provenance is a per-medium table, `PROVENANCE_FIELDS`, read from a **declared** `source_type` | accepted | `validate_knowledge_units` required `{video_id, segment_id, start_sec, end_sec, evidence_excerpt}` unconditionally, and a post claim has no seconds: every correct Twitter unit came back with four `incomplete_provenance` fields plus `invalid_source_timing`, because a bound that is absent is not a number. One table rather than a second validator — the surrounding rules (an id that can become a global id, kind/class agreement, the excerpt being real visible text) are the same rules whatever the medium, and D-185 is what happens when one rule acquires a second implementation. **Declared, not sniffed** from the shape of the first `source` block: a unit that merely *omitted* `start_sec` would otherwise be silently reclassified as a post claim and skip the timing rules altogether. The defaulting rule is the one `library.py` already applies to `metadata.json` — a document that does not say is a YouTube run (`ids.DEFAULT_SOURCE_TYPE`). A post claim carries **no run id**: YouTube's `video_id` guards against a bundle applied to the wrong run, and the Twitter guard is stronger and free, since `validate_post_provenance` requires the `post_id` to be an item of *this* run's capture |
| D-227 | The `twitter` source type is **registered** with a source-only projection, and `adapt_project`'s refusal is left exactly as it was | accepted | `T-227` writes `metadata.json`, and `io.run_dirs` globs `output/*/metadata.json`, so an acquired post became **discoverable** — while `get_adapter` refuses an unregistered source type, so one Twitter run took the whole project's projection down: the `MemoryRepository` oracle, the library, search and the Map, for every YouTube run as well. The first attempt made `adapt_project` skip-and-name, and that was wrong twice: `adapt_project` raising is what `strict=True` reproduces for `T-104`'s equivalence proof (D-043, D-078), and it is also what stops a run claiming the reserved `library:concepts` namespace — a run declaring `source_type: "library"` is refused *precisely because* nothing is registered for it, so making unregistered types skippable would have quietly deleted that guard. Two tests said so immediately. So the registration is the fix, and it carries only what needs no decision `T-228` owns: one `Source` record, no `Entity` (an entity carries a `Locator`, and D-212 hands the widening that lets one express a post id *and* a span to `T-228`), no `Artifact`. `adapter.version` is `0.1` so the completion forces a re-index. The projection is honestly empty rather than absent, and `adapter_metadata` says which task owns the rest |
| D-228 | `SourceAdapter._status` moves to the **base class**, and subclasses override `read_canonical` instead | accepted | It lived on `YouTubeAdapter`, and the Twitter adapter's first version wrote `read_status(validation)` straight into the field — a bare string where the model requires an object with three verbatim copies, two paths and a bounded `audit_attempts`. **Schema-invalid, and nothing caught it**: `check_records` enforces id uniqueness rather than the schema, and no test validated a *Twitter* projection. The same schema's own `audit_attempts` `$comment` records the previous instance of that exact gap — a floor of `1` that made every scaffolded run's record invalid, "which nothing caught because nothing validated a scaffolded run's record". What a `status` record *is* is not a YouTube rule, so it belongs where every adapter inherits it; how a damaged file is *reported* is the adapter's business, so that is the override. `test_the_twitter_source_record_validates_against_the_v1_schema` closes the gap for this medium |
| D-229 | A Twitter run gets an **apply gate**, and the bundle's top-level contract is one implementation for both media | accepted | `prompts/twitter/01_post_extraction.md` tells a model to return an extraction bundle and nothing could apply one: `artifacts.apply_extraction_bundle` reads `transcript.json` and `segments.json` and runs the time-based validators, so the chain ran capture → `initialize_run` → *nothing* → `validate_run`. A model's output would have been written to the canonical files by hand, which is precisely the state the YouTube gate exists to prevent — D-164 and D-169 were both found *at* that gate. `twitter.extract.apply_extraction_bundle` is its counterpart: validate `knowledge_units`, post provenance, relationships, item coverage and the coverage↔unit links, refuse the whole bundle if any of them errors, and write the four canonical files in one `write_group` so a half-applied bundle is never validated as a whole one. What is **not** duplicated is the bundle's own shape — the `units` misspelling (D-073), the three required keys and the silently-defaulted `relationships` (D-169), the unknown-key refusal and `extraction_metadata` being an object are medium-neutral, so they moved to `validators.bundle_shape_error` and both gates call it. It returns a message rather than raising, because the two callers raise different types and only the *rule* is shared |
| D-230 | The apply gate **imposes** what the capture and the pipeline know, rather than accepting the audit's version of it | accepted | The item-based reading of D-164, whose lesson was that a bundle which states its own bound gets measured against itself. Three groups, split by who is entitled to the answer. What the capture says — `basis`, `source_id`, `source_type` and `excluded_items` — is read off the capture: a document that renamed its own `basis` would be read by the *time-window* validator and report every post missing, and one that shortened `excluded_items` would quietly promote a third-party parent into something nobody has to account for. What the pipeline minted — the `source_unavailable` omission (D-225) and the `capture_text_truncated` unresolved item — is re-imposed when an audit drops it, which is what stops a truncated run reaching `PASS` by deleting the gap; `prompts/twitter/05_item_coverage_audit.md` already promised the model these three states were not its to overwrite, and this is where that promise is kept. What the audit says — each entry's `status`, its `knowledge_units`, its own omissions — is untouched, and `coverage_not_audited` is deliberately *not* re-imposed, because resolving it is exactly what an audit does. `summary` is recomputed, never believed |
| D-231 | The Twitter run fixtures are **run directories in a miniature root**, and the evidence is re-homed rather than the capture rewritten | accepted | `evidence_integrity` resolves a capture's recorded evidence paths against `run_dir.parent.parent`, with no parameter for "where the project root is" because a second root-resolution rule is what D-039 removed. So a committed run fixture had exactly one shape available: `tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/` is the output root, `tests/fixtures/` is the project root, and a capture there records `twitter-runs/<case>/raw/<file>`. One level deeper — under an `output/` of its own, the way a real run sits — would also have been invisible to git, since `.gitignore` excludes `output/` at any depth to keep real evidence out of the repository. The measured bytes are copied into each run's own `raw/` and the paths repointed; `sha256_sanitized` is recomputed from the copy and compared, while `sha256_raw` and `sanitization_removed` are **carried**, because they are claims about bytes the provider returned *before* sanitization and those bytes were never committed. Two of §11's case descriptions were written before the decisions that settled them and the fixtures follow the decisions: the edit case names prior ids **on the item and nowhere else** (D-224, not "named omitted"), and the partial-thread capture's excluded member is a **third-party parent** rather than a tombstone |
| D-232 | `copied_timestamp` parses through a **rewrite for the floor interpreter**, and copies the model's own text unchanged | accepted | `requires-python` declares 3.10, and 3.10's `datetime.fromisoformat` is narrower than RFC 3339: it accepted a `Z` designator only from 3.11, and parses fractional seconds of exactly three or six digits. `common.schema.json#/$defs/isoTimestamp` accepts both spellings and `ISO_TIMESTAMP_PATTERN` mirrors it — and **every Twitter capture writes `Z`**, since `acquisition.requested_at` is `…:00Z` and `_metadata` copies it to `metadata.acquired_at`. So `adapt_project` raised `ValueError: Invalid isoformat string` over an acquired post on the floor interpreter and on no other, and the local suite could not see it: the interpreter in daily use here is 3.14, where both spellings parse. Found by CI, on the first push of this branch, which is the argument for the 3.10 row in the matrix. `_parseable` rewrites for the **parse only** — a copied field that quietly changed spelling would be a record stating something the canonical file does not — and the test asserts the rewritten string rather than "it parses here", which on 3.11 and later would prove nothing |
| D-233 | A source claim is projected as a **per-item artifact addressed by the generic `text_span` locator**, and no locator branch is widened. Revises D-212's plan | accepted | D-212 handed `T-228` the widening of the `post_id` branch and called it additive, which it is — but the user stated the source-type order on 2026-09-04 (D-234) and that changes what the choice costs: Medium, articles, books and website links are all coming, and a per-medium branch carrying the same three span fields would be repeated four times in a file whose own header says the reserved branches exist "so that adding PDFs, articles, or posts needs no schema version bump". The generic branch is already the right shape — `artifact_id`, `start_char`, `end_char`, `excerpt` — so a post, an article, a book chapter and a web page are one locator type over four artifact kinds, and the enum already has `article` and `pdf`. **The cost is real and is the reason this is a decision rather than a cleanup:** an artifact per post means a ten-post thread mints ten artifacts, and those artifacts carry no bytes of their own. Both have precedent rather than being invented here — `YouTubeAdapter._video_artifact` is `role: external` with `path: None` and `bytes: None`, and states in its own docstring that `available` means a URL was recorded rather than that anything was fetched. What this *avoids* is the guard with no counterpart: `_locator` addresses the segments artifact only when the provenance names this source's own video and leaves the locator unaddressed otherwise, and a post claim carries no `video_id` to compare — with one artifact per item the artifact id **is** the answer, so nothing has to be chosen. **Correction of the shape as first written:** the id was drafted as `twitter:<anchor>:post:<post_id>`, which the grammar refuses — `globalId` is three parts and `idPart` excludes colons on purpose, "so identifiers split unambiguously". The local segment is colon-free: `post-<post_id>`, alongside `video` and `KU-D-0001`. Canonical files are untouched by any of this: `T-227` froze the unit `source` block and this decision is about the projection only |
| D-234 | The source-type order is **Twitter → Medium/articles → books → website links**, every one of them reaching the **graph and the vault**, and `T-230` owns the finalize/vault generalization for all four | accepted | The user's, stated on 2026-09-04. Two things in it were not derivable from the plan: the **order**, since the Phase 6 row says only "Additional sources (Medium, web pages, others)", and **books**, which no row names at all — the nearest is Phase 5, unscoped. The "and the vault" half is what makes it a decision rather than a note: `artifacts.finalize_run` requires `metadata['video_url']`, `metadata['channel']` and `metadata['transcript_hash']`, and `_obsidian_files` writes `vault/videos/<id>.md` with `type: video` frontmatter — so the wall is not Twitter-shaped, it is YouTube-shaped, and every one of the four hits it. Three homes were possible and the other two were refused for stated reasons: folding it into `T-228` would rewrite an acceptance clause mid-claim ("coexist through adapter, index, API, search, Reader and Map" — the vault is not in it) and design a four-medium generalization under a Twitter-shaped task, and leaving it in `T-229` would have the phase gate *implement* the seam it exists to rehearse. So `T-230` is a task of its own, sized for all media, and it runs **between** `T-228` and `T-229` so the gate still walks a finished journey |
| D-235 | The medium-neutral half of the projection lives on **`SourceAdapter`**, with `_locator` as the one overridable hook | accepted | `_knowledge_entities`, `_derived_refs`, `_canonical_relations` and `_derived_from_relations` were `YouTubeAdapter`'s while it was the only adapter minting anything, and they are identical for both media apart from where the evidence sits. This is D-228's argument applied a second time and at four times the size: `_status` moved to the base because what a status record *is* is not a YouTube rule, and the Twitter adapter had already written a schema-invalid one by re-deriving it. `_derived_refs` is the sharpest case — its docstring exists to explain that the `EntityRef` list and the `derived_from` edges are read from one place so they cannot disagree, and a second copy would have been free to disagree with its own edges. `ArtifactSpec`, `_file_artifact` (with D-100's unmappable-path handling), `list_items`, `required_field`, `build_id` and `edge_id` moved with them. The YouTube projection is unchanged and asserted so: the same 18 artifacts, 2 entities and 2 relations over `pass-run`, compared record for record against a project holding only it |
| D-236 | `check_locator` enforces invariant 3 over **`text_span`** as well, and refuses a non-integer offset | accepted | The function returned early for anything that was not a `time_range`, so the branch `T-228` puts into production had no cross-field check at all: JSON Schema bounds `start_char` and `end_char` at zero and compares neither, so a span ending before it starts was schema-valid and unorderable — which is exactly what invariant 3 exists to refuse. Stricter than the seconds rule in one place, deliberately: a float is accepted as a timestamp and refused as an offset, because `text[0:2.0]` is a `TypeError` and an offset that cannot slice the text it addresses is not an offset. `artifact_id` is required here and on no other branch, and its absence is caught explicitly rather than passing as "nothing to validate". What is **not** checked is the excerpt against the span's length: the two agree only under the medium's own definition of a character, `twitter.extract` already compares an excerpt with its own slice verbatim while holding the capture's text, and a length rule here would fire on the Persian case and nowhere else |
| D-237 | A post artifact is `role: external` with **no path and no bytes**, and `available` is the capture's own `availability.state` | accepted | The text lives in `capture.json`, which is its own artifact; what a post record carries is the *identity* a `text_span` can name. `YouTubeAdapter._video_artifact` already established the shape for a thing that exists remotely — `role: external`, `path: None`, `bytes: None`, `available` stating that a URL was recorded rather than that anything was fetched — so this is that pattern rather than a new one. A **tombstone still gets an artifact**: an unavailable item is a real item of the run, dropping it would put the artifact set at odds with the item coverage that counts it, and `available: true` for it would be the class of claim the capture contract makes unrepresentable. `immutable` is `false` because the bytes are not ours and were never fetched; `raw/` is where this run's immutable evidence sits, one artifact per preserved provider read, discovered from the directory because a capture preserves one file per route and no table can name them in advance |
| D-238 | **No provider name reaches an index record**, and the acquisition block is not carried into `adapter_metadata` | accepted | The `T-228` row says provider names are not part of the UI contract, and `adapter_metadata` is served on `/api/sources` — so copying the capture's `acquisition.provider` there would publish the tool, its version and its binary digest on the read surface, and adding the `T-225` or `T-226` fallback later would then be a *visible change of behaviour* rather than an implementation detail. The provenance is not hidden: `capture.json` is an artifact anyone can open, and it is where the acquisition record belongs. `test_no_provider_name_reaches_the_index` serializes the whole record set and fails on the tool name, the binary digest, or any route name appearing in it |
| D-239 | The Reader and Quick Read render a `text_span`; the other four locator types keep the honest fallback | accepted | Both surfaces read `locator.type === "time_range"` and fell through to `JSON.stringify(locator)` under a comment saying every other type was "reserved in v1 and not produced by any adapter" — true when it was written and false the moment D-233 landed. This is the one place the T-228 row's "only where the generic renderer cannot already represent it" clause bites, and the bidi rules are why: a post's excerpt is the case most likely to be Persian, and `JSON.stringify` renders it as an escaped string with **no direction isolation**, which is precisely the rendering `Bidi` exists to prevent. So the span branch quotes its excerpt through the same `Bidi` blockquote a time range uses, prints its own coordinate (`characters 0–45`, translated) and never seconds — a post has no timeline and a `0:00` would be a coordinate the record does not carry. The four still-reserved types keep the fallback unchanged, and a test asserts they do, so widening two branches did not quietly claim to render six |
| D-240 | **Finalize dispatches on one per-medium table, `artifacts.MEDIUM_PROFILES`, and "which medium is this?" has one answer (`ids.declared_source_type`)** | accepted | `T-230`'s row required the generalization be done once for all four media (D-234) and forbade a second `finalize_run` (D-185). The table is what that comes to: **data where a medium differs by a name** — the note's `type:`, its vault subtree, its frontmatter id key, its backlink label, which metadata field holds the source URL, what the audit's unit is called — and **a callable where it differs by a rule**: the report's `## Metadata` rows, a source claim's citation line, the coverage report's body, and the validator that produces the standing verdict. Adding Medium/articles, books or website links is a row; the code that reads it does not change. Two rows exist because two media exist — a row for a medium with no capture contract, no extraction and no adapter would be an invented output format rather than a generalization, and `test_every_medium_that_can_be_finalized_has_an_adapter` is what keeps the table and the adapter registry from drifting apart. **The three callables are pure**, which is what preserves the discipline the two kept refusals depend on: every line of every file is built before `write_group` is reached, so a medium that refuses to render refuses before the first write rather than half way through it — that is how the missing capture digest is caught, since `canonical_hashes` is an object and the string check in `_checked_metadata` cannot see into it. **Seven places, not the four the row named** (see the claim commit): two metadata fields, the note path and frontmatter, `_unit_markdown`'s provenance line, `_coverage_markdown`'s window loop, the `prune` list, and — the one that would have refused every Twitter run before any of the others could be wrong — `finalize_run` calling `pipeline.validate_run`, which reads a transcript and segments. Both validators write `validation.json` and return the same shape (D-225), which is what lets one profile field stand in for both. And the dispatch **refuses** an unknown medium where `validate_provenance` defaults it to YouTube: that function goes on to report, this one goes on to write files whose names and frontmatter come from the answer. `declared_source_type` moved from `adapters.base` down to `ids`, which owns the vocabulary, because the finalize path needs the same answer and cannot import the index layer without inverting the layering; `library.rebuild_library` held an identical second copy and now calls it. `validate_provenance` deliberately keeps its own read and says so in a comment — it uses `.get(key, default)`, which returns the `None` a document stating `"source_type": null` carries, and that is how it reports `unknown_source_type` instead of silently validating a null as a YouTube run. |
| D-241 | **`artifacts.SECTION_ORDER` groups its kinds in tuples: `report.md` was not reproducible** | accepted | The groups were `set` literals and `finalize_run` iterates them (`for kind in kinds for unit in grouped.get(kind, [])`), so the order of units inside every multi-kind report section was set iteration order over interned strings — which changes with `PYTHONHASHSEED` and therefore from one process to the next. Finalizing the same run twice produced two different files, with no validator looking at `report.md` and nothing in the suite pinning it. **The committed fixtures could not catch it**: `pass-run`, `partial-run` and `fail-run` each hold one `principle` and one `synthesis`, so no section ever holds two kinds, and the 69-unit sample that does expose it lives under gitignored `output/`. Found by trying to *check* `T-230`'s byte-identity clause rather than by reading the code: the before/after comparison disagreed on unit order, and the before/before comparison disagreed with itself. The fix is the order the author already typed. Pinned three ways — the groups must be tuples, the flattened kind order must match across five hash seeds in five subprocesses, and a run whose section holds both `claim` and `principle` (built by driving `initialize_run` and the apply gate, since no fixture has that shape) must finalize to the same bytes under two seeds. |
| D-242 | **A post's note is a note about a post, and a post claim cites the post it was taken from** | accepted | The per-medium half of D-240, stated as the shapes it produces. The note is `vault/posts/<anchor>.md` with `type: post` and `anchor_post_id:`, and a unit note backlinks `Source post: [[<anchor>]]` — the medium is named by `type:` alone, with no second `source_type:` line, because `type: video` already said it for the first medium and a YouTube note has to stay byte-identical. A source claim's citation is **`[post <id>, characters n–m](https://x.com/i/status/<id>)`**: the post's own id and codepoint span, never the run's and never seconds, so a claim taken from the seventh post of a ten-post thread cites the seventh post rather than the anchor — which is the artifact `TwitterAdapter._post_artifacts` mints for that item (D-233, D-237). The wording matches what the Reader renders for the same claim (D-239), so a unit read in the vault and the same unit read in the app do not describe their provenance differently. **No URL is invented**: a capture item carries no `url` field, and the one used is `twitter.extract.post_url`, which already writes `metadata.source_url` — so the note and the capture cannot spell it two ways. The report's integrity row is `Capture hash` over `canonical_hashes['capture.json']`, because there is no transcript and the capture is the one canonical document extraction reads; the audit is `post-by-post` over `coverage.items`. Missing provenance fields are **refused** here rather than defaulted, unlike the YouTube renderer whose `start_sec or 0` is left exactly as it was: that default is unreachable (`validate_post_provenance` requires all three fields and a `FAIL` run is refused before rendering), and it is kept because the acceptance clause pins YouTube rendering byte-for-byte — but *there* the default was reachable, by this renderer not existing. |

## 7. Definition of done and checks

### 7.1 Per task
- Tests proportionate to risk, written and run.
- No canonical file or `raw/` evidence modified.
- Cache changes never reported as data achievements.
- New architectural decisions recorded in §6 and canvas plan §19.

### 7.2 Regression baseline
```bash
.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q               # expect: 3071 passed, 0 failed, 111 subtests (plus new tests)
git diff --stat -- output/                  # expect: empty, always
.venv/bin/python tests/fixtures/runs/build_fixtures.py
git status --porcelain -- tests/fixtures/runs/   # expect: empty — regeneration is byte-identical (D-157)
.venv/bin/python tests/fixtures/captures/build_captures.py
git status --porcelain -- tests/fixtures/captures/  # expect: empty — same rule for the capture fixtures
.venv/bin/python tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/build_fixtures.py
git status --porcelain -- tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/  # expect: empty — and the same rule for the runs built from them
.venv/bin/python tools/generate_api_types.py --check   # expect: types.d.ts is up to date
(cd web && npm ci && npm run typecheck)     # expect: silent — tsc --noEmit, risk R17
(cd web && npm run typecheck:browser && npm run typecheck:scripts)  # the gate's and the scripts' own
(cd web && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build)  # expect: clean; 743 passed, 23 skipped; a clean build
```

`--check` duplicates `tests/test_api_types.py::test_the_committed_declarations_are_current`;
it is listed because it names the fix in its own error message.

**Per-task test counts drift; count them, do not trust them.** The counts quoted in the
§5 task rows are *collected pytest items*, which is what the suite reports and what
parametrisation multiplies — not the number of `def test_` lines, which is much smaller
(106 collected from 24 definitions in `tests/test_index_schemas.py`, 132 from 43 in
`tests/test_ids.py`). Re-derive one without running anything:

```bash
.venv/bin/python -m pytest --collect-only -q tests/test_index_schemas.py | tail -1
```

Every count in §5 and §7.2 is a snapshot. Phase 1 fans out to four tracks and Track D
owns `tests/`, so these numbers move under any session that reads them. Treat a mismatch
as staleness in this file, not as a regression, and re-run the command before quoting a
figure. Every count in this file moved twice in a single day: `test_index_schemas.py` went
58 → 83 → 106 and `test_ids.py` 51 → 67 → 132 across two waves of agents. The figures here
were counted this way after the second wave, 2026-08-31.

CI runs all of these on every push and pull request
([`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml)), across **nine
jobs**. This table said "five" and listed five, four rows after `lint`,
`integration`, `browser` and `requirements` had joined the file (D-187).

| Job | What it proves |
|---|---|
| `tests` | The suite on Python **3.10, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14** on Linux and 3.12 on macOS (D-115), installed as `.[dev,legacy,ui]`. 3.10 is the `requires-python` floor; 3.14 is the interpreter in daily use here and was missing, so every number measured locally came from a version CI never ran |
| `zero-dependency` | The core package installs and tests with **no** extras (ADR 0001 invariant 5), and fails if any optional distribution declared in `pyproject.toml` creeps into that install — the list is derived from the file rather than restated, because a hand-kept one named 7 of 14 (D-157) |
| `extras` | All **five** declared extras — `youtube`, `mcp`, `ui`, `legacy`, `dev` — each installed, `pip check`ed, imported, and run against the suite. `dev` and `legacy` were the only two any job installed, so three of the five could have been broken with nothing to say so. `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py::test_ci_installs_every_declared_extra` fails if an extra is added to `pyproject.toml` without a row here, and the `mcp` row additionally asserts the server builds with 10 tools against the installed `mcp` |
| `fixtures` | The committed run fixtures regenerate byte-identically (`T-006`, R11), checked with `git status --porcelain` because `git diff` cannot see a file the generator newly wrote (D-157) |
| `web-typecheck` | `tsc --noEmit` over `web/` and the generated declarations (R17, D-038), plus the browser gate's own program, which also carries `vite.config.ts` (D-156); then `npm test` and `npm run build` |
| `lint` | `ruff check` and `mypy` over `src/x2knwldg`, installed as `.[dev,legacy,ui,mcp]` — with `mcp`, because without it the one module that fails the check was the one module the check could not see (D-155) |
| `integration` | The frontend's integration tests against a real `create_app` over the committed fixtures (D-116) |
| `browser` | The Map in Google Chrome over the built bundle and the real API — `T-209`'s 31 behavioural specs plus `T-215`'s 16 visual-quality scenarios, **47** in all |
| `requirements` | `requirements.txt` installs, because a forward nobody follows is a forward nobody has checked (D-111) |

**The zero-dependency check (`T-009`), reproducible locally:**

```bash
python3 -m venv /tmp/bare && /tmp/bare/bin/pip install . pytest
for p in jsonschema openapi-spec-validator networkx pyvis yt-dlp fastapi uvicorn \
         mcp youtube-transcript-api requests httpx ruff mypy; do
  /tmp/bare/bin/pip show "$p" >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "LEAKED: $p"
done                                        # expect: no output
/tmp/bare/bin/python -m pytest -q           # expect: 1862 passed, 533 skipped, 111 subtests
```

The 496 skips are 491 API/HTTP-layer tests, which belong to the `ui` extra, plus the
`jsonschema`, `mcp` and `legacy` tests — all of them skipping by design, and all of them
counted here for the first time (this block still quoted 1438/5 from before the API layer
existed, which §7.2's own warning is about). Every `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py` test runs
here — the scaffold's own guards must hold on the install they are about.

> **Run this one. It is not a formality.** Re-measured 2026-08-31 for the first time
> since the suite was 515, it **failed** — and the failure was in the suite, not the
> package. `test_no_captions_asks_for_a_transcript` monkeypatched
> `youtube.process_youtube_url`, but `process` probes `YOUTUBE_DEPENDENCIES` *before* it
> calls the fetch and refuses outright when none of them import — correct behaviour, and
> the subject of its own test. So on the install ADR 0001 invariant 5 is *about*, the test
> never reached the branch it names: stdout was empty and `json.loads` raised. Its
> neighbour `test_a_name_collision_is_not_reported_as_transcript_required` was worse — it
> **passed for the wrong reason**, because the missing-extra error satisfies all four of
> its assertions without ever reaching the collision. Both now pin the probe through
> `_pretend_the_youtube_extra_is_installed`, and the collision test asserts the message is
> *not* the missing-extra one. The lesson generalises: **a test that monkeypatches past a
> dependency probe must pin the probe**, or it silently tests nothing on the install with
> no extras. The full-extras suite cannot see this class of defect — it was green at 1348
> throughout.

### 7.3 Re-verify the numbers in §3
```bash
python3 -c "import json;print(len(json.load(open('output/pqlWNihgdjI/knowledge_units.json'))['units']))"
python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('output/pqlWNihgdjI/coverage.json'))['status'])"
python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('output/pqlWNihgdjI/validation.json'))['status'])"
cat output/library/status.json
# status.json now reports runs_discovered / runs_indexed / runs_skipped / skipped_runs[]
# / incomplete_runs[] beside the counts, and each videos.json entry carries problems[]
# (D-043). Read runs_indexed, not videos, for "how many runs are in the library".
# NOTE: the committed output/library/ predates D-043 and will change on the next rebuild.

# The frozen API surface (T-005): 11 GET endpoints, and every payload a $ref into schemas/v1/.
python3 -c "
import json; d = json.load(open('schemas/api/v1/openapi.json'))
print(len(d['paths']), 'paths;', len(d['components']['schemas']), 'components')
print(sorted({m for ops in d['paths'].values() for m in ops}))"

# The same numbers, reached independently through the adapter (T-004).
.venv/bin/python -c "
from pathlib import Path
from x2knwldg.adapters import adapt_project
print({k: len(v) for k, v in adapt_project(Path('.')).by_model().items()})"

# And once more through the T-007 seam, which is what the API will call.
.venv/bin/python -c "
from pathlib import Path
from x2knwldg.repository import MemoryRepository
print(MemoryRepository.from_project(Path('.')).status().payload()['counts'])"

# And a fourth time through the SQLite index, which is what a route will call
# once T-105 wires it. Writes .x2knwldg/ and nothing else; delete it freely.
.venv/bin/python -c "
from pathlib import Path
from x2knwldg.index import build_index, SqliteRepository
from x2knwldg.index.search import document_indexer, search_retrieval
from x2knwldg.repository import SearchQuery
root = Path('.')
build_index(root, index_documents=document_indexer(root))
repo = SqliteRepository.open(root, search=search_retrieval)
print(repo.status().payload()['counts'])
print({q: repo.search(SearchQuery(q=q, limit=1)).total for q in ('learning', 'model', 'the')})"
# expect: the same 1/85/86/118, and {'learning': 4, 'model': 19, 'the': 258}
```

### 7.4 End-to-end scenarios (canvas plan §17.3)

**Walked 2026-09-02** against `x2knwldg ui` serving the three committed fixtures, driven
through a real headless browser rather than through jsdom — the point of the exercise being
to test what a person meets, not what a test harness meets. It earned its keep twice:
scenario 2 was unwalkable on the first attempt because search returned nothing at all
(D-068), and then failed a second time because the jump was discarded at the click (D-069).
2141 passing tests had reported neither. Both are fixed and the scenario was re-walked;
each now has a test that fails without its fix.

| # | Scenario | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a source with `PARTIAL` status and see the real warning | ✅ **pass** — `Overall/Validation/Coverage: PARTIAL`, `audit_attempts: 3`, both file paths cited, and the panel states in the UI that the values are copied and never raised toward `PASS`. `fail-run` shows `Validation: FAIL` beside `Coverage: PASS` unreconciled, which is what R11's fixture exists to prove |
| 2 | Search a transcript phrase and **jump to the timestamp** | ✅ **pass** — after D-068 (search returned nothing at all) and D-069 (the jump was lost at the click). Re-walked: the hit links to `#/sources/…?tab=transcript&t=30`, the Reader opens on **Transcript**, and the caption covering `0:30` is scrolled to and marked with a rail, a weight change and `aria-current="location"`. The URL is the state, so the position survives a reload and can be shared. A malformed or negative `t` is ignored rather than seeking to zero, and an unknown `tab` falls back to Overview — both checked in the browser |
| 3 | Select a knowledge unit and see its actual evidence | ✅ **pass** — statement, evidence excerpt, locator (`0:00 – 0:30`, `segment_id`, `artifact_id`), `Play from here`, and the canonical file named. The derived unit reads *No locator is recorded for this unit* rather than borrowing one, and the two classes differ by glyph (◆/◇), label, **and** rail style (solid/dashed), not by colour alone |
| 4 | Move an entity from Map to Canvas | ⛔ **out of scope** — Phase 2/3 |
| 5 | Create a user relation without touching any canonical file | ⛔ **out of scope** — Phase 3 |
| 6 | Draw with the pen, reload, strokes survive | ⛔ **out of scope** — Phase 4 |
| 7 | Delete the cache, rebuild, boards intact | ◐ **half met** — the cache half passes: `.x2knwldg/` deleted, the command rebuilt it on the next start to identical counts, search still answered, and nothing under `output/` was modified. *Boards intact* has nothing to assert against until Phase 3 |

Also checked while the browser was open, because both are Phase 1 claims that only a
rendered page can settle: the Persian switch flips `lang`/`dir` to `fa`/`rtl` and the layout
mirrors — sidebar, tabs and every label/value pair — while Latin identifiers, paths and URLs
stay LTR inside it (D-012, D-014); and the embed stays a facade, naming its host and
requesting nothing until asked (D-061).

**Phase 1's gate is met** for every scenario the phase owns: 1, 2 and 3 pass, and 7's cache
half passes with *boards intact* left to Phase 3. Scenarios 4–6 belong to Phases 2–4.

The lesson worth carrying into Phase 2: both defects lived in the seam between components
that were each correct and each well tested. `refresh_index` worked and the CLI did not ask
it for a corpus; the Reader could seek and the hit did not tell it where. A suite of unit
tests cannot see a gap that exists only between two of its subjects — walking the product
can, and did.

---

## 8. Agent parallelism model

### 8.1 Phase 0 is a serialization point

Phase 0 produces the entity, ID, and API contracts that every other track consumes. **Running parallel agents during Phase 0 guarantees rework.** One focused agent, start to finish.

### 8.2 After the Phase 0 gate — four tracks, disjoint file ownership

Exclusive file ownership is the mechanism that makes concurrent agents safe. An agent writes **only** inside its own paths.

| Track | Owns exclusively | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| **A — Indexer** ✅ **done** | `src/x2knwldg/index/` | SQLite schema, migrations, scanner, FTS5, incremental hashing — behind `repository.IndexRepository`. Delivered 2026-09-01 (`T-101`–`T-104`); see [`index/README.md`](../src/x2knwldg/index/README.md) |
| **B — API** | `src/x2knwldg/server/` | FastAPI routes, path safety, range requests, versioned responses |
| **C — Frontend** ✅ **done** | `web/` | Vite/React scaffold, tokens, i18n/bidi shell, Library, Reader. Delivered 2026-09-02 (`T-109`–`T-114`); see [`web/README.md`](../web/README.md) |
| **D — Fixtures & tests** ✅ **done** | `tests/` | Labeled `PARTIAL`/`FAIL` fixtures, contract tests, rebuild equivalence. Delivered 2026-09-02 (`T-115`) |

**What makes A, B, and C genuinely concurrent:** the API contract is frozen in `T-005` and TypeScript types are generated from it, so Track C develops against a mock and never waits on Track B. Tracks A and B meet only at the repository interface fixed in `T-007`, which now exists:
[`IndexRepository`](../src/x2knwldg/repository/README.md) with a working reference
implementation behind it, so **Track B can start before Track A finishes**.

### 8.3 Contention points — integrator only

These files are small, shared, and merge-hostile. **No track agent edits them.**

[`.github/CODEOWNERS`](../.github/CODEOWNERS) encodes this table and §8.2, so a PR that
crosses a boundary asks for a review instead of merging quietly. It is a tripwire, not a
lock: §8.2 is still the rule. Change the paths there and here together, never one alone.

- `src/x2knwldg/cli.py` — the `ui` subcommand. `T-008` left a refusing stub there; `T-116`
  replaced its body (2026-09-02) and no other task touches it. Note that `T-116` also added
  `src/x2knwldg/server/serve.py`: Track B's directory, entered by the integrator **after**
  Track B was complete, because D-055 forbids `cli.py` from importing the `ui` extra at all
- `pyproject.toml` — the `ui` extra
- `.gitignore`
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — small, shared, and merge-hostile. Track C may change what
  `npm run typecheck` covers, but the job itself is integrator-owned
- `docs/` — including this file
- `src/x2knwldg/library.py` — single owner, load-bearing for `kg_navigator` (see §6)
- `src/x2knwldg/repository/base.py` — the A↔B contract. Widening it is a contract change
  (`schemas/api/v1/openapi.json` first, ADR 0002 second), not a track's local decision

### 8.4 What must stay sequential

- **Phase 3 Canvas and Phase 4 pen.** Both mutate the same React Flow surface and pointer-event handling. Run sequentially, or in isolated git worktrees with a deliberate merge.
- **Schema migrations.** One writer, versioned, ever.
- **`library.py` ID generation.** Single owner.

### 8.5 Recommended concurrency

**3–4 agents maximum.** That is what the track split naturally supports. Beyond it, agents contend on §8.3 files and coordination cost exceeds the speedup.

### 8.6 Phase 2 ownership and safe fan-out

`T-202`, `T-203` and `T-204` were the serialization points, and all three are done: the
renderer major, the Graphology shape, the pagination merge rule and the renderer lifecycle are
decided, and later tasks inherit them rather than re-deciding them. URL identity is still one
decision to make, in `T-206`.

**The fan-out ran, and it is now closed.** `T-205` and `T-206` were built in parallel on
disjoint files and integrated by hand; `T-207` then ran alone, because it touches every
surface the two of them left, and `T-208` ran alone after it for the same reason. What
remains nothing: `T-209`, the gate, was not parallelizable either — it is a walk through the
whole route — and it has run.

- ~~`T-205`~~ owned the style/reducer/legend modules, the semantic-density policy and their
  tests. Done.
- ~~`T-206`~~ owned `mapLink`, search/preview/Peek/focus history state and their tests. Done,
  and its selection contract is `mapLink`'s `focus` parameter plus `useMapFocus.focusEntity`.
- ~~`T-207`~~ owned the bounded on-stage card overlay, the neighbourhood/related list and
  Quick Read, and it was the task that added the renderer's **event and coordinate
  adapters**. Done: the canvas is a third caller of `useMapFocus.focusEntity` and
  `useMapPeek`, not a second identity, and `MapSession` grew `onNode`/`onRender`/
  `graphToViewport` plus a mutable handler slot — no second renderer, no second graph
  store, no second style table, no second card formatter (D-134–D-138).
- ~~`T-208`~~ owned the honest-state reducers, the DOM companion, the one disclosure, the
  motion policy, the responsive/touch rules and the bidi pass — and it *moved* two things
  rather than adding them beside their originals: the transient Peek is now rendered by the
  route (a card inside a folded panel is invisible), and the route's server stub moved to
  `src/test/mapServer.ts` because the route grew a second suite. Done.

**What the integration settled**, and `T-207` then relied on:

- One cutter, `cutToBudget` in `labelPolicy.ts`, serves both the WebGL label and the DOM
  card. They differ only in how they *admit* the cut — an ellipsis on a canvas that has
  nowhere else to put it, words beside the text in a card. The DOM card's own cutter was a
  second one and a worse one: it counted UTF-16 units and would halve a surrogate pair.
- A focus the loaded pages do not hold styles **nothing**. `hasFocus` dims everything
  unrelated, so honouring a URL focus that is not on screen would dim the whole picture
  around an absence. The rail says so in words instead.
- Every catalogue key is spelled out at its use site, never built from a template. The
  scaffold guard finds a shipped string by its literal, and a computed key makes a live
  translation read as abandoned.

The integrator alone edits `web/package.json`, `web/package-lock.json`, `web/src/App.tsx`,
`web/src/components/Shell.tsx`, the shared message catalogues, global styles, CI, and `docs/`.
`web/src/map/mapSession.ts`, `web/src/map/sigmaRenderer.ts` and `web/src/views/MapView.tsx`
are the shell `T-204` left: `T-205` passes reducers into `createSigmaRenderer`, `T-206`
passes URL state into `MapView`, and `T-207` extended the existing session boundary with the
event/coordinate adapters D-132 needs — none of them constructs a renderer of its own, and
`sigmaRenderer.ts` is still the only module that names Sigma. What `T-207` added there is an
*adapter* over the instance rather than the instance itself, because Sigma's emitter is
typed per event name and spelling those names anywhere else would pull `sigma`'s types back
into the application's module graph (D-127).
`T-208` then integrated accessibility, disclosure, motion and bidi across those shared
surfaces — adding a second reducer to `mapState.ts` rather than a branch to each site, and
adding the camera's animation argument to the *existing* `MapCamera` boundary rather than a
second renderer. `T-209` was the serialized phase gate and it has run: it added no surface to
the application at all, and its three changes to one are `MapSession.frame` on the *existing*
renderer boundary (D-146), the release of a refused container's context inside the existing
`attach` (D-147), and the route reading Escape on `window` instead of on its own element
(D-148). The gate itself lives outside `web/src`, in `web/browser/`, and imports nothing from
it. Later tasks must not add a second graph store, selection grammar, style table,
card-content formatter, Sigma lifecycle wrapper, disclosure element or reader of the
reduced-motion query.

### 8.7 Phase 2.1 was a visual serialization point (closed)

~~`T-211` is the only task that may start.~~ It owned mockups and the visual specification,
changed no production UI, and ended at an explicit user approval (D-191). Starting
implementation while that gate was open would have repeated the failure `T-210` exists to
correct.

`T-212`, `T-213` and `T-214` have now run, alone and in that order, and each is the reason
the next had to wait: `T-212` recomposed `MapView`'s whole return, the workspace half of
`base.css` and `Shell`'s frame; `T-213` replaced everything inside the field it built —
`constellation.ts` entirely, the overlay component, the Focus half of `base.css` and the
route's own frame subscription; `T-214` then dressed what those two placed, and touched the
label policy, the style table's view state and the shared badge module to do it. `T-215` has
now run too, alone, and it was the last gate-only task: a gate is a walk through the whole
route and cannot be split either. It changed no application file — the measurement moved out
of a script into `browser/composition.ts`, sixteen scenarios were written over it, and what is
outstanding is the acceptance rather than any code (D-195).

`T-216` has now run too, alone, and for the same reason its four predecessors did: it rewrote
how every mark on the field is sized (`mapStyle.ts`, `sigmaRenderer.ts`), re-derived the label
policy that depends on that size (`labelPolicy.ts`), changed three of the surfaces floating on
the field and removed a fourth (`MapView.tsx`, `MapSearchRail.tsx`), and added a clause to the
gate that measures all of it — so a second agent in any one of those files would have been
resolving conflicts rather than composing. It also had to adapt four specs outside the visual
gate, because folding a surface moves the controls inside it: `renderer.spec.ts` opens the
filter panel before driving a filter, `states.spec.ts` and `walk.spec.ts` open the account
before pressing its own "load more" button, and `access.spec.ts` presses Enter on the search
rail's trigger during its keyboard walk, which is what a reader with no pointer now does below
the `full` tier. One more change was a *consequence* rather than an adaptation: `findMark` in
`gate.ts` hunts for a mark by sweeping the pointer over a grid of the stage, and a mark sized
in screen pixels is a fifth of the area a framing-scaled one was over the seven-node fixtures
— so the sweep's step and budget are now a function of the size the style table actually
draws, and the two specs that hunt for a mark pass over both libraries again.

**The serialization point is closed.** The acceptance was given on 2026-09-03 (D-202), so all
six children pass and `T-210` is done. What the phase leaves behind for the tasks after it is
one rule rather than six: the Map's composition has an approved reference, an instrument that
measures both sides of it, and four measured differences standing in `SPEC.md` §17 — six since D-203, which added two of its own there. A later
task that touches `mapStyle.ts`, `labelPolicy.ts`, `base.css` or `MapView`'s return is not
free to move a card, a mark's size or a floating surface's footprint without re-running
`visual.spec.ts` and saying what moved: the recorded numbers are a regression surface, which
is what D-193 and D-201 made them.

The existing integration boundaries remain binding: one `GraphSnapshot`, `mapLink`, selection
identity, card formatter, Sigma lifecycle, honest-state reducer, disclosure and reduced-motion
reader. `T-213` calculated presentation positions for Focus and they are not graph records:
they flow into no snapshot and no canonical file, and `placeOrbit` is a pure function of the
field, the neighbourhood and the chrome's measured rectangles. `T-214` inherited that
constants table and grew no second one — its own additions are a field on `MapViewState`, one
badge component and rules in `base.css`. `T-215` added no application surface at all: like
`T-209`, the gate lives outside `web/src` and imports nothing from it, and `git diff` over
`web/src/` and `output/` is empty for it.

### 8.8 Phase 2.2 starts serially, then fans out only at the provider boundary

`T-221`, `T-222`, `T-223` and `T-224` are complete, and `T-227` is claimed. The default
provider path exists, so `T-225` and `T-226` are now genuinely optional fallbacks rather than
the only way forward, and `T-227` has the "at least one passing acquisition task" its
dependency asks for. D-220 removed the one thing that could have re-ordered them.
`T-222` owned the qualification matrix and report and changed no production integration.

Its result is the case for this ordering rather than a formality about it. The candidate's own
`x fields tweet` table disagreed with measurement in four places, and the thread capability the
MVP sentence assumed turned out to exist in one direction only. A schema frozen on the
advertised response would have promised whole threads while dropping seven posts in ten.

`T-222` recorded its go/no-go and `T-223` froze the one canonical Twitter capture contract
(D-212), so the gate that held provider work is open and it may fan out:

- `T-224` owns the qualified local provider seam (`x-cli`, which the spike passed) — **done**.
- `T-225` owns the opt-in FxTwitter fallback and official oEmbed corroboration. Not a
  dependency of `T-227` (D-220); it raises `text.completeness` to `corroborated` and adds URL
  spans to captures acquired after it lands.
- `T-226` owns passive Firefox capture and its local importer.

Those three tasks may run in parallel only if their files and fixtures are disjoint and every
one writes the `T-223` contract rather than extending it privately. `T-227` then serializes
extraction, provenance and coverage; `T-228` serializes adapter/index/UI coexistence; `T-229`
is the full phase gate and runs alone. Provider credentials, browser sessions, `output/` raw
evidence and the existing YouTube sample are never shared scratch space.

## 9. Risks — delta from canvas plan §18

| Risk | Status | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| **R7** No valid graph data for development | ✅ **Resolved** | Real `PASS` extraction exists: 69 units, 56 relations, 17 concepts |
| **R11** No `PARTIAL`/`FAIL` fixture, so honest-status UI is untestable | ✅ **Resolved** | `T-006` delivered `tests/fixtures/runs/{pass,partial,fail}-run`, labelled test-only. `fail-run` is deliberately awkward: report, graph, and Obsidian export all exist while `validation.json` says `FAIL` |
| **R12** Dual ID vocabulary (2-part in library files, 3-part in index) drifts | 🟢 **Mitigated** | `T-003` made the two forms exact inverses and round-trip tested them; `T-004` removed the remaining f-string risk on the index side — every id in `src/x2knwldg/adapters/` is built through `ids.py`, and `base.check_records` re-asserts all three invariants at production, not only in tests. `adapt_library` also refuses a concept whose stated `global_id` disagrees with its `library_id` |
| **R13** `finalize_run` triggers a full `rebuild_library` over *all* sources every time — cost grows linearly | 🟡 Watch | Acceptable at current scale; revisit when source count grows. Do not fix speculatively |
| **R14** No path-traversal guard in existing `query.py` / `mcp_server.py` joins | 🟢 **Mitigated** | Every join now goes through `pipeline.resolve_run_dir`, which rejects rather than sanitises (D-020), with traversal tests in `tests/test_core_pipeline.py::RunLookupTests`. `T-108` must use the same resolver for HTTP path parameters — [ADR 0003](adr/0003-reject-unsafe-identifiers.md) makes that binding and supersedes ADR 0001 invariant 8, which named the rewriting sanitiser instead |
| **R15** Absolute host paths baked into `library/status.json` and `library/videos.json` | 🟢 **Mitigated** | `library.py` emits an additive `relative_path` beside each absolute `path`, and `T-004` closed the reading side structurally: every path in an index record goes through `adapters.project_relative`, which **refuses** a path outside the project root rather than storing it. `adapt_library` does not read `status.json` or `videos.json` at all. Tested by `test_no_record_carries_an_absolute_path` and `test_a_run_outside_the_project_root_is_refused` |
| **R16** `value` field on statistic units is polymorphic (`int` \| `list[float]`) | 🟡 Watch | Cannot map to one SQL column — store as JSON text, keep the canonical file authoritative. `EntityRef` has no `value` field and `additionalProperties: false`, so the adapter cannot carry it into the index by accident |
| **R17** Nothing in CI proves the generated `types.d.ts` is *valid* TypeScript | ✅ **Resolved** | `T-008` brought Node into CI: the `web-typecheck` job runs `npm ci && npm run typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`). Two details make it real rather than decorative, and both are asserted by `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py`: `web/tsconfig.json` keeps `skipLibCheck: false`, without which `tsc` skips every `.d.ts` — including the only file the job exists to check — and lists `../schemas/api/v1/types.d.ts` as a root file so it is checked whether or not anything imports it. Verified by injecting a bad type into the generated file and watching `tsc` fail (D-038). **Turning `skipLibCheck` on reopens this risk silently** |
| **R18** D-028's additive search fields exist only as a test helper | ✅ **Resolved** | `T-007` moved them into `MemoryRepository.as_api_hit`, which is the code path `T-106` serves and the one `tests/test_api_contract.py` now exercises — one implementation, not two. It also stopped hard-coding `youtube`: the source type is read from the indexed source, so a hit from a source the index does not hold gets `source_id: null` and `global_id: null` rather than an address that resolves to nothing |
| **R19** Sigma v4 is prerelease and feature combinations may expose renderer defects | 🟢 **Mitigated — gate passed** | `T-202` pinned one exact v4 beta and a compatible Graphology set, then exercised create/update/resize/select/teardown and 21 create/kill cycles on the real 86-node/118-edge graph in Chrome 152 on this MacBook: no uncaught error, no page failure, and every WebGL context released. The v4 line is **kept** and the stable-v3 fallback was not needed. Two beta-specific observations remain live rather than closed, both in ADR 0005 § *Gate result*: the renderer emits `GL_INVALID_OPERATION` warnings from `glDrawArraysInstanced` on context setup while drawing correctly, and its published declarations need `@types/events` to type-check at all (v3 shares that dependency, so it is not a v4 defect). The pin is the mitigation that stays: an upgrade off `4.0.0-beta.5` is a deliberate act that re-runs this walk, not an `npm install` side effect (D-117) |
| **R20** Readable cards obscure the topology or recreate an HTML node renderer at graph scale | 🟢 **Mitigated — the policy is code and the completeness path is tested** | D-132 permits one primary selected card, one transient Peek and only density-budgeted neighbour previews on the stage; WebGL keeps every loaded entity as a mark, and the semantic related list exposes every neighbour returned by the bounded API even when its card cannot fit. `T-205` stated the label density policy; `T-207` made the *card* policy a function — [`placeConstellation`](../web/src/map/constellation.ts), four clauses each returning a counted reason — and proved the accounting is total: cards placed plus omissions counted equals the neighbours returned, on the real fan-out as well as on fixtures. The overlay holds no control and no focusable element (D-137), so it cannot become the node renderer this risk names, and cards are not re-placed per frame (D-138). `T-209` measured it on the real route and the policy needed correcting in the *other* direction: the grid refused 7 of 8 cards that would have fitted while placing two that overlapped, so the clause is now an overlap test over the card's measured footprint in four orientations (D-145), and a selection frames its own neighbourhood so the cards have room to be placed at all (D-146). The accounting is asserted in the browser as well as in the suite — cards placed plus omissions counted equals the neighbours the server returned — and no two drawn cards share a pixel |
| **R21** Functional completion is mistaken for visual acceptance | ✅ **Resolved 2026-09-03 (D-202)** — six children delivered, the gate green over all of them, and the acceptance given by a person rather than inferred from a suite | `T-210` is a separate release-quality epic. `T-211` delivered the approved compositions (D-191), `T-212` built the workspace (D-192), `T-213` built the Focus composition inside it (D-193) and `T-214` dressed it (D-194) — and every one of them showed the risk behaving exactly as predicted. The mockups' own geometry checks caught four defects a passing component suite would not have seen; `T-212`'s green jsdom suite said nothing about the three the browser found; `T-213`'s green 607-test suite said nothing about **five** more, each of which a reader would have seen immediately — a reserved card box smaller than the card Chrome drew, the focused card sliding under the counts float, a tier whose boxes did not fit its own minimum width, a relation pill wider than the run it had to sit in, and every card on the wrong half of the field because direction was read from the neighbour's end of the relation; and `T-214` closed a clause that had been *stated* since ADR 0006 and shipped broken through two tasks, because no test asserted it. All of it came from looking at a running build. `T-215` then built the gate — sixteen scenarios, the recorded numbers held, every geometry clause zero (D-195) — and proved the risk's own thesis a fifth time from the other side: **everything it asserts is green, and the pictures still differ from the approved ones in three ways no assertion fires on** (`SPEC.md` §16). Explore's marks scale with the camera, so the quiet field of the reference is quiet only at 1440; the `compact` tier's floats are panels where the reference has chips; both drawer panels are mounted with nothing focused. The risk closes when the user accepts the captures — or when a remediation task makes them acceptable and this gate re-runs green over it. A green suite, including this one, cannot close it. The user opened `T-216` rather than accepting, which is the risk working as designed: the gate said green, a person said not yet, and the difference between those two sentences is what R21 has always been about. **`T-216` has now run, and it is the sixth demonstration and the sharpest.** Its largest clause came with a decision D-196 had already reasoned out — move to `"screen"` sizes, and D-122's zoom rule retires with them — and *both halves of that reasoning were wrong about the library*: the ratio function it proposed clamping instead cannot see the field at all, so that alternative could never have worked, and `"screen"` does not freeze size under zoom, so the rule it was going to retire is still standing. Only reading `scaleSize` and re-running the captures found either, which is this risk's own lesson one level up — at a decision rather than at a defect. R21 closed on 2026-09-03 when the user accepted the captures (D-202) — and it closed on the only evidence that could ever have closed it. What it leaves behind is the machinery it forced into existence: an approved reference set that is regenerable from committed sources, sixteen scenarios whose recorded numbers are a regression surface, a chrome-share bound read off the reference by the instrument that measures the build, and four measured differences standing in `SPEC.md` §17 — six since D-203, which added two of its own there rather than remembered. A later surface that repeats this mistake will be caught by those and not by this row |
| **R22** A Focus layout becomes a second graph/data truth | 🟡 **Mitigated by design; verify in implementation** | D-152 limits Directional Orbit to presentation derived from the existing `GraphSnapshot`, selection and neighbourhood. It may position records differently, but may not mint identities, merge neighbourhood data into the snapshot, add an endpoint or store a second graph; returning to Explore is deterministic |
| **R23** `x-cli` or another public surface fails from Iran or changes without notice | 🔴 **Open — blocks integration** | `T-222` tests the exact pinned release and alternatives from the target environment, records raw evidence and failure modes, and produces a capability table. No candidate becomes a dependency from documentation alone |
| **R24** A provider succeeds but returns an incomplete or misordered thread | 🔴 **Open — contract risk** | Provider success and completeness are separate fields. `T-223` models omissions/tombstones and item-based coverage; `T-227` makes `PASS` impossible while an expected item is unaccounted for. Passive capture is `PARTIAL` unless a boundary is proved |
| **R25** X credentials/session material leak or a fallback silently discloses requested posts | 🟢 **Mitigated by boundary; verify in spike and implementation** | No password, cookie, token, profile or session export is accepted. FxTwitter is explicit opt-in with fixed origin; Firefox capture strips session data and sends no extra request. Security fixtures and logs are audited before the phase gate |
| **R26** Unofficial-service, X Terms or AGPL obligations are obscured by a working demo | 🟡 **Accepted/contained** | ADR 0007 states that unofficial paths are not represented as X-approved. `x-cli` remains an external optional tool until its licence boundary is explicitly settled; third-party network use is disclosed; Treasury/twscrape automation and account-pool patterns are out of scope |

Risks 1–6 and 8 from canvas plan §18 remain as written.
