## 10. Reuse — do not reimplement these

| Existing code | Use it for |
|---|---|
| `io.write_json` | **Already atomic** (same-dir temp + `os.replace`) — satisfies canvas plan §15 outright. Note: Markdown/report writes use plain `write_text` and are *not* atomic |
| `io.sha256_file` | Incremental index change detection |
| `io.timestamp_url` | YouTube deep links; `&t=<int>s` output is contract-locked by `tests/test_core_pipeline.py` |
| `pipeline.validate_run` | The **only** legitimate source of run status. Read it; never recompute |
| `pipeline.resolve_run_dir` | Resolve a run directory from an externally supplied id. It **rejects** an unsafe id; use it for every HTTP path parameter (D-020, [ADR 0003](adr/0003-reject-unsafe-identifiers.md)) |
| `pipeline._safe_identifier` | Guard the id a run is *created* at. **Never for a lookup, and never for an id that arrived from outside the process** — that is `resolve_run_dir`'s job, because only it also proves the resolved path stays under the output root. [ADR 0003](adr/0003-reject-unsafe-identifiers.md) forbids substituting any other check for it |
| `pipeline.project_root` (`T-008`) | Resolve the project root: explicit argument, then `X2KNWLDG_PROJECT_ROOT`, then the working directory. The MCP server and the `ui` command both call it. Do not re-read the env var anywhere else (D-039) |
| `cli.VERDICT_EXIT_CODES` / `cli.verdict_exit_code` | The one mapping from a run verdict to an exit code, and the `EXIT_*` constants beside it. Never write an integer literal for an exit code, and never give a new meaning to `2` — it belongs to `argparse` (D-040). The table is in [`README.md` § Exit codes](../README.md#exit-codes) and in `x2knwldg --help` |
| `constants.MAX_AUDIT_ATTEMPTS` | The three-attempt coverage-repair cap. `validators.validate_coverage` enforces it against the required `coverage.json` `audit_attempts` field; do not re-state the number anywhere else |
| `constants.SEGMENT_TARGET_SEC` / `SEGMENT_MIN_SEC` / `SEGMENT_MAX_SEC` / `SEGMENT_OVERLAP_SEC` | The segmentation timing contract, and coupled: `OVERLAP < MIN <= TARGET <= MAX`. `segmenter.create_segments` defaults to them and re-checks the relation on whatever it is actually given; `pipeline.import_transcript` takes `TARGET` and `OVERLAP` from here too. Never write one of these numbers as a literal |
| `constants.COVERAGE_WINDOW_SEC` | The coverage window width (`coverage.create_pending_coverage`). It is written into `coverage.json` as `window_size_sec`, so **its type is part of the file format** — keep it an `int`, or every stored document gains a `.0` |
| `constants.MAX_CAPTION_GAP_SEC` | The largest silence between captions that is not reported as a transcript gap (`transcripts.transcript_integrity`) |
| `constants.TIME_TOLERANCE_SEC` | The **one** epsilon for comparing two times in seconds, used throughout `validators`. Timestamps are parsed from millisecond text and round-tripped through JSON, so an exact `==` is a coin flip. There were six copies of this constant; six chances for two comparisons to disagree about whether the same pair of times matches. Do not write a seventh |
| `io.read_json` / `io.read_json_or_reason` | The package's **two** JSON readers, and there were five. `read_json` is strict and raises `JsonReadError` naming what is wrong — use it where a damaged file means stop. `read_json_or_reason` returns `(document, None)` or `(None, reason)` — use it where the caller must carry on and *state* the damage, as `library.rebuild_library` does. There is one sibling, for the callers that must also tell *absent* from *broken*: `adapters.base.read_optional_json_or_reason` returns `(None, None)` for a file that is not there and a reason only for one that is there and will not read (`read_optional_json` is now just its first element). So: `io.read_json` when the caller must stop, `io.read_json_or_reason` when it must carry on, the adapters' sibling when it must also distinguish "not there" from "broken". Do not add a sixth reader, and do not catch `json.JSONDecodeError` directly |
| `cli.LOOPBACK_HOSTS` (`T-008`) | The accepted bind addresses. `T-116` kept the check *before* the dependency probe, because otherwise the invariant lapses on any machine without the `ui` extra — the machines where it is least likely to be noticed. `server.serve.bind` resolves these three names rather than re-deciding them, and `test_every_accepted_loopback_host_can_actually_be_bound` proves all three are bindable, so none is accepted-but-unusable |
| `library.rebuild_library` | Generalize **additively**; do not rewrite. Its `status.json` now reports `runs_discovered`, `runs_indexed`, `runs_skipped`, `skipped_runs[]` and `incomplete_runs[]` beside the counts, and every `videos.json` entry carries `problems: []` — read `runs_indexed`, never `videos`, when you mean "how many runs are in the library" (D-043) |
| `query.search_knowledge` | Its two result shapes are the de-facto API contract to preserve while FTS5 replaces the linear scan |
| `ids.py` (`T-003`) | Every identifier operation: build, parse, convert between the global and library forms, and enforce the three cross-field invariants. Never assemble an id with an f-string |
| `adapters/` (`T-004`) | The **only** way to turn a canonical run into index records. `adapt_run` for one run, `adapt_library` for the cross-source concepts, `adapt_project` for everything — the scan `T-102` makes incremental. Pass `hash_artifacts=True` for `io.sha256_file` digests. Do not re-read canonical files into ad-hoc dicts elsewhere |
| `Source.adapter_metadata.unmappable_artifacts` / `.unreadable_files` | The adapters' two diagnostic channels, and the answer to the "silent drop" finding that recurs through this audit (D-045). `unmappable_artifacts`: a generated `vault/` note whose filename cannot spell an id, skipped and named. `unreadable_files`: a canonical file present but damaged, named so a missing count is not read as a zero. Each carries `path` + `reason`, is free-form by schema, and is **absent** when there is nothing to report — an empty list reads like an unread finding. A UI showing a source must surface both rather than let the omission disappear between the run and the Reader |
| `adapters.project_relative` | Every path that reaches an index record or an API response. It refuses a path outside the project root, which is what keeps risk R15 closed |
| `twitter/provider.py` (`T-224`) | Reaching the pinned `x-cli`. `verify` is the **only** way in and it hashes the file before executing it; its keyword arguments exist so a test can pin a stub, not so a caller can accept whatever it found. `read_tweet` builds the argv, and `_classify` is the one place an exit status becomes a contract `outcome` — including D-214's finding that exit `8` is the whole transport class. `TIER0`/`GUEST` carry the route and surface names; `DEFAULT_TIER` is `GUEST` because D-207 says so. Do not add a subcommand to `ALLOWED_SUBCOMMANDS` without a decision: it is what makes `x auth import` unreachable |
| `twitter/evidence.py` (`T-224`) | Preserving acquisition bytes, for **every** provider. `sanitize` then `scan_for_credentials` — in that order, because the spike's stripper missed an escaped ampersand once and a redactor is not evidence that redaction worked — then two digests, and a refusal if bytes already at the path differ. `prepare` returns the text unwritten so the caller can hand it to `io.write_group` with the capture: a run lands whole or not at all. `T-225` and `T-226` must not write their own redactor |
| `twitter/normalize.py` (`T-224`, D-219) | Turning one provider record into one capture item, and the **single** implementation of it — `tests/fixtures/captures/build_captures.py` imports it rather than keeping a copy. Spans are codepoint offsets and every one is re-sliced before it is kept; an absent field stays absent rather than becoming `[]`. A second provider adds a *reader* that produces this shape, never a second copy of this |
| `twitter/acquire.py` (`T-224`) | The orchestration: `parse_reference` (offline and strict — the one place a user's URL becomes an id), the upward walk, and the capture assembly with every honesty rule the schema cannot state. `TransientFailure` versus `ProviderDrift` is D-214's distinction and is load-bearing: a transient failure writes **nothing**, so a dropped tunnel can never look like a thread that ends there. A capture is written once; re-acquiring into an existing one is refused |
| `schemas/api/v1/openapi.json` (`T-005`) | The **specification** for `T-105`–`T-108`, not a suggestion. Eleven `GET` endpoints, response bodies `$ref`-ing `schemas/v1/`. Do not add an endpoint, a field, or a status code without editing this document and its tests first |
| `schemas/api/v1/types.d.ts` (`T-005`) | The frontend's types. Import it; never hand-edit it. Regenerate with `python tools/generate_api_types.py` |
| `web/src/map/seedPositions.ts` (`T-202`) | Every Map node's starting position. Seeds are a hash of the node's `global_id`, **never** its index in a page: a Map accumulates pages (D-118), so an index-derived seed moves a node when the next page arrives and makes the layout unreproducible across reloads. It also guarantees no node starts at the origin, which ForceAtlas2 answers with `NaN` rather than with an error. Apply it where a node is *inserted*, not in a pass before layout — `graphology-layout-forceatlas2` reads a missing `x` straight into a `Float32Array`, so a forgotten seed is an invisible missing node, not a crash |
| `web/src/map/graphProjection.ts` (`T-203`) | Turning an `EntityRef` or an `IndexedRelation` into graph attributes, and deciding whether a repeated identity is a repeat or a conflict. A node's attributes are `x`, `y` and the record **verbatim** — no label, size or colour, because styling is the renderer's reducers (D-124) and D-122 forbids drawing the raw `label` anyway. `recordDifference` is the comparison, and it reads an absent field and a `null` one as the same statement (D-125); anything else raises `GraphConflictError` naming the field |
| `web/src/map/graphSnapshot.ts` (`T-203`) | **The** graph store. Pages accumulate into one `MultiDirectedGraph`; nodes dedupe by `global_id`, edges by `id`; a D-059 edge whose far endpoint has not arrived is held, never drawn and never dropped; a page that conflicts is refused before anything is inserted. `state()` is what a view may say out loud — loaded, held, known total, `hasMore`, `lastPageTruncated`, and `complete` under D-123. Do not add a second store, and do not compute completeness anywhere else |
| `web/src/map/graphWalk.ts` (`T-203`) | Driving that store: `open` retires the walk in flight and starts a new snapshot with its own graph, `loadMore` is deliberate, `cancel` keeps what was drawn, and a page that answers after its question was replaced is dropped whole (D-079's lesson). Cursors are carried and handed back, never parsed (ADR 0005 invariant 6). `apiGraphPages` is the loader over the typed client, so an undeclared filter is a compile error, and `GRAPH_PAGE_LIMIT` is the contract's maximum, guarded by `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py` |
| `web/src/map/mapSession.ts` (`T-204`) | The **one** renderer lifecycle: lay out, draw, resize, zoom, reset, kill. A second `attach` kills the first, `kill` is idempotent, and every operation after a kill is a no-op rather than a throw — because a `ResizeObserver` callback or a page that merged while the route was closing arrives exactly there. `creates` and `kills` must finish equal after any sequence, which is how a leaked WebGL context shows up as a number instead of as a blank canvas somewhere unrelated (D-126). Do not construct a renderer anywhere else; §8.6 forbids a second wrapper and `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py` fails if one appears |
| `web/src/map/sigmaRenderer.ts` (`T-204`) | The only place the application names Sigma's constructor, and the only module that imports `sigma` at all. Reached through a **dynamic** `import` from the Map route: a static one is a `ReferenceError` in jsdom and fails suites that have nothing to do with the Map (D-127). Its three stated settings are decisions, not defaults — `allowInvalidContainer: false` (D-129), `renderLabels: false` until D-122's policy exists, and `enableEdgeEvents: false` until `T-207` selects an edge. `T-205`'s reducers are passed in here |
| `web/src/map/useGraphWalk.ts` (`T-204`) | Binding `GraphWalk` to a component: `deps` name the values that make it a different question (the `useAsync`/`usePaged` convention), a change opens a new snapshot, and unmount disposes the walk. It never calls `loadMore` itself — a continuation is deliberate (D-118) — and it never reports the graph as a comparable value, because the graph is mutated in place and `snapshotId` (a new graph) and `pagesApplied` (more of the same one) are the two facts a renderer can key off |
| `web/src/map/neighbourhood.ts` (`T-207`) | Turning one `/api/graph/neighborhood/{id}` response into the **complete** related list: hops from the centre by an undirected walk over the returned edges, each relation's own direction, and a deterministic order (hops, then the stated relation, then the identity — never a score). It is projected through `T-203`'s `nodeAttributes`/`edgeAttributes`/`recordDifference`, so a self-contradicting response is refused rather than merged, and it is **not** a second graph store: nothing here accumulates a page, and the neighbourhood is never handed to a renderer or merged into the drawn snapshot (D-134). `MAP_DEPTH_MIN`/`MAP_DEPTH_MAX` are the contract's own bounds, guarded by `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py`, and `parseDepth` **ignores** a value outside them rather than clamping it |
| `web/src/map/constellation.ts` (`T-207`) | D-132's density policy, as a pure function. `placeConstellation` decides which cards the stage may carry and returns a *counted reason* for every neighbour it refuses — not drawn, off the stage, crowded, over budget — so an omission is never silent (R20). The numbers (`MAP_STAGE_CARD_BUDGET`, `MAP_STAGE_CARD_BOX`, `MAP_STAGE_PRIMARY_BOX`, `MAP_STAGE_CARD_GAP`, `MAP_STAGE_CARD_INSET`, `MAP_STAGE_SETTLE_MS`) are stated here once and `T-209` measured every one of them in a browser: the two boxes are what Chrome laid a card out at, and they replace the square grid cell that refused the cards which fitted and placed the ones that overlapped (D-145). Crowding is an **overlap** test over the drawn rectangle -- the card, the gap and the mark it points at -- tried in four stated orientations before a refusal, so a card opens the other way rather than covering its neighbour. Do not place a card anywhere else, and do not add a second budget: the label budget is `MAP_LABEL_NEIGHBOUR_BUDGET` and it is deliberately a different number for a different thing. `T-212` added one optional input, `obstacles` — the floating chrome's own rectangles, in the stage's coordinates, because the workspace put the controls *on* the field and a card is drawn over whatever the route puts there (D-137). They are **measured** by the route and never stated as insets: the composition mirrors under `dir="rtl"`, and an inset per edge mirrors an already-mirrored coordinate (D-191, D-192). A card refused for one is `no_room`, and the selected card chooses between three tiers — clear of the chrome, then merely on the stage, then its preferred direction — because being covered is worse for the one card D-132 guarantees than for any other |
| `web/src/map/useNeighbourhood.ts` (`T-207`) | The two requests a selection makes, bound to a component. The halves are caught **separately** so a readable entity is not lost to a failed list and a `404` on the entity is stated as what it is — the id names nothing — while anything that is not an `ApiFailure` (an `AbortError` above all) is rethrown so `useAsync` still drops a retired question whole. `deps` are the focus and the depth; nothing is requested with nothing selected |
| `web/src/map/mapSession.ts` — the adapters (`T-207`) | `onNode`, `onRender`, `graphToViewport` and `nodePosition`, plus `setHandlers`. The handlers are a **mutable slot** read by a trampoline subscribed once per renderer, because a session rebuilt to pick up a new closure would kill the live renderer and the accumulated picture with it. `nodePosition` answers `null` rather than a guess for anything it cannot place. Report to the view; never decide: `onSelectNode` must reach `useMapFocus.focusEntity` and nothing else, or there are two selection identities |
| `web/src/components/MapRelation.tsx` (`T-207`) | The one place a connection is named: the relation verbatim, the endpoints in the record's own `from → to` order (so direction is reading order, not an arrow alone), and the vocabulary and provenance as badges. The stage card, the related row and Quick Read all use it, so one edge cannot be described two ways |
| `web/src/map/mapState.ts` (`T-208`) | What the Map is **allowed to say about itself**: `describeGraph` over the walk's report, `describeCanvas` over the renderer's. Two total functions, no state, every number copied from `GraphSnapshotState`. The pairs are the point — unasked is not empty, partial is not whole, refused is not empty, undrawn is not absent (D-139–D-141) — so a new state is a clause here, never a fresh condition at a render site. It holds no message text: the catalogue key is chosen at the use site, because a computed key defeats the guard that tells a shipped string from an abandoned one |
| `web/src/map/outline.ts` (`T-208`) | The drawn graph as a list: `outlineOfGraph` walks the accumulated graph in **insertion order** (which is the API's order — never a ranking, invariant 15), formats each row through `previewOfEntity`, counts drawn relations at the mark with a self-relation counted *once*, and states what its bound did not list. The one place the DOM companion's contents are decided; do not sort it, and do not window the list that renders it (D-142) |
| `web/src/map/motion.ts` (`T-208`) | The **only** reader of `prefers-reduced-motion` in the application, and the reason it exists is that the stylesheet cannot reach a camera animated in script on a canvas. `cameraAnimation()` answers `{ duration: 0 }` or `undefined` — never a duration of its own, which would override the renderer's and add a fifth unmeasured Map number. Read at the gesture, never cached: the preference can change while the page is open (D-144) |
| `web/src/components/Disclosure.tsx` (`T-208`) | The **one** collapsible panel: `<details>`/`<summary>`, the heading inside the summary, the panel's own count beside it, and `preferOpen` as a *preference* the reader can overrule. `open` is written as a prop once at mount and imperatively afterwards, because a `<details>` fires `toggle` a task late and rendering `open={state}` let a stale event close a panel nobody had touched (D-143). A second `<details>` anywhere in `web/src` fails a guard |
| `web/src/components/MapOutline.tsx` (`T-208`) | The DOM companion itself, over `outlineOfGraph` and `MapResultCard` — the same card the search rail and the related list render, so a row's Focus button is the same selection identity a mark is. It opens itself when there is no picture, and it is not windowed. Add a surface the canvas has and the DOM does not, and this is where it belongs |
| `web/src/map/mapSession.ts` — framing (`T-209`) | `frame(centreId, neighbours)` and the two numbers it needs (`MAP_FOCUS_MARGIN`, `MAP_FOCUS_MIN_RATIO`). It is the only thing that moves the camera to a *place* rather than by a step, and it exists because selection and the camera had never spoken: a focus was framed nowhere and `zoomIn` zooms about the middle of the stage, so pressing it pushed the selection off screen (D-146). It frames the focus **and its neighbours in the drawn graph**, once per selection, and it asks the renderer where a node is in *framed* coordinates (`nodeDisplay`) rather than converting graph units itself. The margin is calibrated over 23 real focuses; a renderer upgrade re-runs that table |
| `web/browser/` (`T-209`) | The **browser gate**: `gate.ts` holds what every spec shares and `walk`/`states`/`access`/`renderer` hold the walk itself. Development-only, outside `web/src`, and it imports nothing from the application — a spec that imported `MAP_STAGE_CARD_BOX` would agree with whatever the module says, which is the one thing a gate must not do. Every expected number is read back out of the payload the page was answered with, so one gate serves the real library and the fixtures. `tests/test_ui_scaffold.py` fails if it leaks into the application, if `npm test` starts collecting it, if its pin becomes a range or if CI stops running it |
| `web/playwright.config.ts` (`T-209`) | What the gate is pointed at, and why each half is real: the **built bundle** through `vite preview` (because `x2knwldg ui` serves `dist/` and the renderer is a lazy chunk, D-127) over the **real API** through `scripts/dev_api.py`. `X2KNWLDG_BROWSER_PROJECT_ROOT` chooses the library, `X2KNWLDG_BROWSER_CHANNEL` the browser. One worker, no retries: a retry hides the flake a gate exists to find |
| `web/src/test/mapServer.ts` (`T-208`) | The route's **one** stub server and its sized stage, shared by `MapView.test.tsx` and `MapView.access.test.tsx`. Routed by path, with the *empty honest answers* as defaults — no sources, a `404` for an entity, an empty neighbourhood, a counted-zero search — so a test that relies on a fixture has to say so. Two copies of this were two chances for one suite to assert against a server that answers unlike the other |
| `web/src/test/mapRenderer.ts` (`T-207`) | The **one** fake behind `MapRendererFactory`. It records the lifecycle, keeps the handlers the session subscribed so a test can fire a real `clickNode`, and resolves a coordinate back to the node that holds it so a test can say where a *node* is. Three private copies of this fake were three chances to assert against a renderer that behaves unlike the others |
| `web/src/api/contract.ts` (`T-008`) | The **only** place `web/` names the generated declarations. Frontend code imports API types from here, never by reaching up the tree (D-038) |
| `web/src/lib/readerLink.ts` (`T-116`, D-069) | The Reader's URL grammar — `readerPath` to build one, `parseTab`/`parseSeconds` to read one, `captionIndexAt` to resolve an offset to a caption. Anything linking into the Reader calls it rather than assembling a path, because a grammar written in one place and read in another is two implementations. `parseSeconds` **ignores** what it cannot read; do not make it return `0` |
| `repository/` (`T-007`) | The **only** thing `T-105`–`T-108` read. No route opens a database, a canonical file, or a run directory. `T-101`–`T-104` implement `IndexRepository` over SQLite without widening it; `MemoryRepository` is what Track B builds against until they do, and the oracle `T-104` proves equivalence against |
| `repository.encode_cursor` / `decode_cursor` | The one cursor encoding. The SQLite implementation issues its keyset cursors through it so both implementations produce the same token for the same position |
| `repository.matches_entity` / `matches_relation` / `matches_source` / `relation_belongs_to_source` | The definition of every filter the contract exposes. Where a SQL `WHERE` clause disagrees with them, they are right |
| `repository.graph_nodes` | The **one** rule for which nodes a source's graph is drawn over — an entity of the source, or an endpoint of a relation `relation_belongs_to_source` accepts (D-041, [ADR 0004](adr/0004-graph-membership-and-search-corpus.md)). `T-101`–`T-104` inherit it; do not re-derive it in SQL |
| `query.run_documents` / `query.rank_documents` | The searchable form of a run, and the ranking over it. `MemoryRepository` builds its corpus from these once per instance (D-042) and `query.search_knowledge` is the CLI's caller of the same pair. `T-103` replaced the corpus, **not** these shapes: `index.search` retrieves candidates and hands them to `rank_documents` unchanged, so the scoring rule has exactly one implementation (D-046). Widening `run_documents`' `searchable` join widens the index too, and needs no second edit: `refresh_index` re-indexes every source on each pass, so the change lands even when no file under `output/` moved. What it *does* move is `T-104`'s measured search totals, which are constants in two test files — expect to update them, and read D-047 before adding a field, because the question is precision rather than recall |
| `constants.py` | The real controlled vocabulary: 22 source kinds, 8 derived kinds, 16 relation types, 11 omission reasons — `source_unavailable` joined them for `T-227` (D-225) |
| `artifacts.SECTION_ORDER` | A ready-made 12-section UI grouping taxonomy for knowledge kinds |

**Conventions to follow:** lazy optional imports inside the dispatch branches of `cli.main` (and `cli._run_process`) so the core stays zero-dependency · `pipeline.project_root` as the **only** root resolution — it reads `X2KNWLDG_PROJECT_ROOT` itself, and D-039 removed the second read from `mcp_server.py`, so no other module may re-read the env var · `config/*.local.json` tracked-example / ignored-local pattern.

**Citation rule (adopted after the Phase 0 audit).** Cite a **symbol**, never a `file:line`. The audit found roughly 3 in 5 line citations across these documents already stale after one session, and three more rotted while its findings were being fixed — one of them mid-session. A line number rots silently and sends the reader somewhere plausible and wrong; a symbol that moves or disappears makes `grep` return nothing, so the staleness announces itself. `tests/test_docs_citations.py` enforces this over every Markdown file in the repository. A line whose subject *is* a rotted citation — ADR 0003 exists partly because `_safe_identifier`'s citation had become a blank line — carries an HTML comment marking it as history — the `citation:history` marker `tests/test_docs_citations.py` defines — and is allowed.

**Vocabulary the Map must style:** `derived_from` and `expresses_concept` are library-only synthetic relations that are **not** in `RELATION_TYPES`. In the current data they are the two most common edges (45 and 17 of 118).

---
## 11. Next step

**`T-230` is done, and `T-229` is the last task in Phase 2.2.** Phase 2.2's default path runs from a
URL to a validated run at the library level: `T-222` measured the routes (D-205–D-211),
`T-223` froze the capture contract (D-212), `T-224` built the seam that writes it and verified
it live over the tunnel (D-213–D-219), and `T-227` turns a capture into canonical outputs the
existing source-grounded / derived pipeline validates — including the **apply gate** a model's
extraction goes through (D-229, D-230) and eight committed run fixtures built by driving that
code (D-231). What is left downstream is `T-228`'s: entities, artifacts, locators, the SQLite
rebuild and the read surfaces.

**Two gaps `T-227` named are now owned rather than noted.** Nothing in `twitter.extract` is
reachable from the CLI — `x2knwldg capture` writes a capture and stops, so initializing,
applying and validating a Twitter run is library-only today; and a Twitter run ends at
`validation.json`, because `artifacts.finalize_run` reads a transcript and segments and has no
counterpart for this medium. Neither is a defect in what `T-227` delivered. Claiming `T-228`
measured the second one exactly, so it is a scope decision rather than a discovery:
`finalize_run` reads `metadata['video_url']`, `metadata['channel']` and
`metadata['transcript_hash']`, and a Twitter `metadata.json` carries `source_url` and
`canonical_hashes` instead. D-234 gives that one to `T-230` and leaves the CLI
gap with `T-229`, so neither is now a gap two tasks each read as the other's.

**Claiming `T-230` re-measured that wall and it is one field narrower and two places wider
than either row says.** `channel` *is* present in a Twitter `metadata.json` — it holds the
author username — so the missing fields are `video_url` and `transcript_hash` alone, and
`_checked_video_id` passes unmodified because a Twitter run reuses `metadata['video_id']` as
its run id. What neither row names is the pair that would not have raised: `_unit_markdown`
defaults `start_sec` to `0` and links `timestamp_url(video_id, start)`, so a Twitter claim
would print `[00:00:00–00:00:00]` against a YouTube watch URL built from a post id — a
fabricated timestamp and a fabricated source URL — and `_coverage_markdown` iterates
`windows`, which an item-based coverage document does not have. `library.rebuild_library`
needs nothing: it already resolves `source_type` per run and reads metadata through `.get`.

**The ordering question is settled (D-220).** A source claim cites a **post id and a text
span** — what the `T-227` row and ADR 0007's MVP both ask for — so the missing URL spans that
D-218 measured are not a prerequisite. `T-225` stays a genuine fallback and lands additively:
`text.entities` is optional and `textEntity.kind` already accepts `url`.

### What is built, and what is left

| Built | Where |
|---|---|
| Run initialization from a capture: `metadata.json` and an item-based `coverage.json` | `twitter.extract.initialize_run` |
| Evidence integrity — the recorded digest, the preserved bytes, and **item re-derivation** standing in for a recomputation a provider read cannot support | `twitter.extract.evidence_integrity` |
| Post provenance: a `post_id` plus a codepoint span whose excerpt **is** its own slice, compared verbatim | `validators.validate_post_provenance` |
| Item coverage and its links, with the genuinely shared rules shared rather than copied | `validators.validate_item_coverage`, `validate_item_coverage_links`, `_audit_attempt_errors` |
| The medium dispatch, and the one thing a post claim does *not* carry | `validators.PROVENANCE_FIELDS` (D-226) |
| Prompts for the two passes that do not survive the change of medium | `prompts/twitter/` |
| A source-only registration, so one acquired post cannot refuse the whole project | `adapters.twitter.TwitterAdapter` (D-227) |
| The **apply gate**: a bundle that fails validation is refused rather than written, and what the capture and the pipeline know is imposed on the audit rather than accepted from it | `twitter.extract.apply_extraction_bundle` (D-229, D-230) |
| The bundle's top-level contract, one implementation for both media | `validators.bundle_shape_error` (D-229) |
| Eight run fixtures, built by driving that code and asserted byte-identical on rebuild | [`tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/`](../tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/README.md) (D-231) |
| The projection every medium shares — entities, their `derived_from` refs, canonical and synthetic relations, and one canonical file artifact — with `_locator` the one overridable hook | `adapters.base.SourceAdapter` (D-235) |
| A Twitter run's artifacts: the canonical files, one per preserved provider read, and **one per capture item** | `adapters.twitter.TwitterAdapter` (D-233, D-237) |
| A source claim's location, as a `text_span` into the post it was taken from | `adapters.twitter.TwitterAdapter._locator` (D-233) |
| Invariant 3 over the other coordinate: a span that ends before it starts, and an offset that cannot slice a string | `ids.check_locator` (D-236) |
| A span rendered as a span — the excerpt through `Bidi`, its own coordinate, never seconds | `web/src/components/EntityCard.tsx`, `MapQuickRead.tsx` (D-239) |
| Two media in one project, through adapter, index, repository, search and graph | `tests/test_twitter_coexistence.py` |
| The finalize dispatch: names as data, rules as pure callables, one row per medium | `artifacts.MEDIUM_PROFILES` (D-240) |
| One answer to "which medium is this?", in the module that owns the vocabulary | `ids.declared_source_type` (D-240) |
| A post's note, its frontmatter and its backlink; a post claim's citation and the post-by-post audit | `artifacts._twitter_provenance_lines`, `_item_sections`, `_twitter_metadata_lines` (D-242) |
| `report.md` reproducible — the same run finalized twice is the same file | `artifacts.SECTION_ORDER` (D-241) |
| Two media finalizing in one project, into one library | `tests/test_finalize_media.py` |

`T-228` did entities, artifacts and locators, the SQLite rebuild and the read surfaces —
and **not** by the widening D-212 planned: D-233 replaced it, so `schemas/v1/locator.schema.json`
gained nothing and the `post_id` branch is still reserved and still unused. `T-230` did finalize
and the vault (D-240–D-242): the wall was seven places rather than the four its row named, the
seventh being that the path called `pipeline.validate_run`, which reads a transcript. Measuring
its byte-identity clause is what found D-241 — `report.md` was not reproducible, because
`SECTION_ORDER` grouped its kinds in sets and `finalize_run` iterates them.

**`T-229` owns what is left: `WORKFLOW.md`, the CLI, the capability table and the failure
rehearsal.** Nothing in `twitter.extract` or the Twitter finalize path is reachable from the CLI
— `x2knwldg capture` writes a capture and stops — so initializing, applying, validating and now
finalizing a Twitter run is library-only. That is the last gap between a URL and a note in the
vault, and it is the one the phase gate walks.

### What claiming `T-228` measured

Four things, read off the tree on 2026-09-04 rather than inferred, so the shape of the work is
known before it starts. None of them is a decision — they are the facts the decisions will be
made against.

1. **The locator widening is a mapping, not an invention.** The unit `source` block
   `twitter.extract` writes carries exactly `post_id`, `start_char`, `end_char` and
   `evidence_excerpt` — which is the widened `post_id` branch, field for field. What has **no**
   counterpart is the step before it: `YouTubeAdapter._locator` addresses the segments artifact
   only when `provenance["video_id"]` equals the source's own external id, and *leaves the
   locator unaddressed* when it does not, so a canonically broken unit is never pointed at the
   wrong artifact. A post claim names no `video_id`, so there is nothing to compare and the
   artifact must be **chosen**. That choice is the guard's replacement and needs to be as
   deliberate as the guard was.
2. **`capture.json` is the analogue of YouTube's `segments` artifact, and no artifact kind can
   say so.** The excerpt is a slice of `text.canonical` of an item inside `capture.json`, which
   is exactly why the segments file rather than the transcript is what a YouTube locator
   addresses. `artifact.schema.json`'s `kind` enum has eighteen members and none of them is
   `capture`. In the other direction, `CANONICAL_ARTIFACTS` names `transcript`, `segments`,
   `graph` and `report` — a Twitter run has none of the four, and the fixtures confirm it: a run
   directory is `capture.json`, `raw/`, `knowledge_units.json`, `relationships.json`,
   `coverage.json`, `validation.json`, `metadata.json` and `work/`.
3. **Both widenings are regenerations, not schema edits.** `artifact.schema.json` is `$ref`ed
   from `openapi.json` twice, `types.d.ts` is committed, and
   `test_the_committed_declarations_are_current` fails if the two disagree — while
   `test_the_served_surface_is_exactly_the_frozen_one` fails if the app's document drifts from
   the frozen one. The locator is not `$ref`ed by any operation but is declared in `types.d.ts`
   all the same, and `test_locator_is_a_discriminated_union` is a live assertion over the branch
   being widened. Adding an enum member and adding optional branch fields are both additive and
   stay in `v1` — the `SkippedRun` precedent (D-050) — but nothing lands as a lone edit to a
   schema file.
4. **`TwitterAdapter.version` is `0.1` on purpose and completing the projection must move it.**
   D-227 says so in as many words: the shape is deliberately partial and a re-index has to be
   forced when this task finishes. Leaving it at `0.1` would let a stale cache serve a
   source-only projection of a run that now has entities.

**Both questions the claim raised are now answered, and by the user rather than by
implementation.** Finding 1's *the artifact must be chosen* is answered by **not having to
choose**: D-233 mints one artifact per item and addresses it with the generic `text_span`
branch, so the artifact id **is** what the missing `video_id` comparison would have
established, no locator branch is widened, and the same locator serves an article, a book
chapter and a web page. Findings 2 and 3 shrink accordingly — `artifact.schema.json` needs a
member for a post rather than for a capture, and `article` and `pdf` are already there for what
follows. Finding 4 is untouched: the adapter version still has to move.

**And the finalize gap is `T-230`'s (D-234).** It is not a fifth item of this task's scope and
not the first of `T-229`'s: `finalize_run` requires `metadata['video_url']`,
`metadata['channel']` and `metadata['transcript_hash']` and `_obsidian_files` writes
`vault/videos/<id>.md` with `type: video`, so the wall is **YouTube-shaped, not
Twitter-shaped** — Medium, books and website links all hit the same four places. Generalizing
it once, in its own task, sized for four media, is what the user's stated order makes the
cheaper of the two.

### What `T-227` owns

| Piece | The rule it must hold |
|---|---|
| **Item-based segmentation** | A post is the segment. `segmenter.create_segments` is time-aware over captions and does not apply — do not force a capture through it, and do not invent a boundary inside a post's text |
| **Order** | Root-first, from `order.basis: parent_links`, never arrival order. Where `completeness.upward.status` is `incomplete` the first item is **not** a root and keeps the `parent_post_id` that proves it (D-217) |
| **Locators** | A source claim names a `post_id` and a codepoint span into `text.canonical` — the authored form, `t.co` links intact (D-211). Not the rendered form, and not a re-expanded URL, because one measured expansion was *longer* than the link it replaced |
| **Quotes** | A separate cited source relation, never embedded content (ADR 0007 decision 8). The capture carries `quote.quoted_post_id` and its author and nothing else — extraction may not fetch the quoted post |
| **Exclusions** | Third-party replies and unavailable items are **named**, with the reason the capture already states. An `availability.state: unavailable` item carries no author, timestamp or text, and extraction may not supply one |
| **Coverage** | Item-based: every included post is covered, omitted with a reason, or unresolved. `coverage.create_pending_coverage` mints *time* windows and does not apply — the second implementation of "what a window is" is the thing to avoid |
| **Metrics** | Absent unless carried as an explicitly time-stamped observation. The contract requires `metrics.observed_at` for exactly this reason; linked pages are never fetched |
| **Text completeness** | `unverified` on every capture this task consumes (D-220). Extraction must not read that as a defect, and must not launder it into a claim |

Validators that recompute digests and enforce locators, order and coverage, with `PASS`
impossible while an expected item is unaccounted for. Raw evidence stays byte-identical:
`output/<id>/raw/` is write-once and `capture.json` is immutable, which
`twitter.acquire.acquire` already refuses to overwrite.

### The eight fixture cases, built

A fixture is a **run directory** under `tests/fixtures/twitter-runs/<case>/` — the input
`capture.json` and `raw/` beside the canonical extraction outputs — **built by driving the real
extraction code**, the way `tests/fixtures/runs/build_fixtures.py` drives the real pipeline
(`T-006`, D-157). Hand-written expected JSON is what lets a fixture drift from what the code
writes. Re-running the builder leaves `git status` clean, and
`test_re_running_the_builder_is_byte_identical` rebuilds every case into a temporary tree and
compares the bytes rather than trusting that. D-231 records the layout the one root-resolution
rule forced, and the two descriptions below that were written before the decisions that
settled them — the edit case names prior ids **on the item and nowhere else** (D-224), and the
partial-thread capture's excluded member is a **third-party parent**, not a tombstone.

Seven of the eight inputs are committed captures derived from measured bytes. Only the `edits`
case is constructed in-test, because a capture in `tests/fixtures/captures/` is a claim about
what a provider returned and no route produces an edit history (D-222); the dangling chain was
measured live rather than constructed, and found a defect while it was at it (D-221, D-223). The
table says which, and what each case exists to prevent rather than merely to exercise.

| Case | Input capture | What it prevents |
|---|---|---|
| **single-post** | `pass-single-post-en.json` | The baseline: a claim carrying a `post_id`, a codepoint span and an excerpt that re-slices exactly, before a thread complicates it |
| **Persian/RTL** | `pass-single-post-fa.json` | 418 characters carrying ZWNJ, Persian digits, a **NBSP** and paragraph breaks. Any normalization or trimming on the excerpt path breaks the re-slice *here and nowhere else* |
| **Persian/RTL + LTR run** | `pass-single-post-fa-video.json` — **built** | The only bidi case with an embedded LTR `t.co` link, and the only media with **no `alt_text`**: extraction must not claim to know what the video shows |
| **complete self-thread** | `pass-thread-terminal-anchor.json` | Root-first over ten items from `parent_links`, first item with **no** `parent_post_id`. Order derived from parent links, never arrival order |
| **partial thread** | `partial-thread-dangling-chain.json` — **measured live** (D-221) | The mirror, and the one place extraction can silently lie: a truncated chain presented as though it began at a root. Also the tombstone-inside-a-thread shape |
| **edit** | `capture_shapes.edited_post_capture()` — **built** in-test (D-222, D-224) | An edit history read as content, or the prior ids fetched. Extraction cites `text.canonical` only and names them omitted |
| **tombstone** | `fail-unavailable-post.json` | Zero source claims and nothing invented from an item with no author, timestamp or text — and `FAIL` surviving into the canonical outputs |
| **quote** | `pass-quote-post.json` | A quoted post becoming embedded content or a fetch. It is a separate cited source relation (ADR 0007 decision 8); doubles as the mention-span passthrough case |

### Four things the fixtures pin, found while planning them

1. **`validate_knowledge_units` hard-requires `{video_id, segment_id, start_sec, end_sec, evidence_excerpt}`** for every source unit, unconditionally, and a post claim has no seconds. That `required` set becomes medium-dispatched **inside the one validator** — a second validator for the same rule is what D-185 is about.
2. **A Twitter run has no `segments.json`.** The capture's `items` array *is* the segmentation, so `segmenter.create_segments` is not reached and no second answer to "what a segment is" is written. `SEALED_CANONICAL_FILES` gains `capture.json` instead.
3. **The provenance check gets stronger.** `validate_provenance` asks whether the excerpt appears *somewhere* in its segment; a capture carries exact codepoint text, so here it is whether the excerpt **is** `text.canonical[start_char:end_char]`. The fixtures pin the exact form, not the substring form.
4. **No locator branch can express a Twitter claim.** `schemas/v1/locator.schema.json` reserves `post_id` with no span fields and `text_span` with no post id — D-212 hands that widening to `T-228`, so these fixtures must state the pipeline-side source shape precisely enough that `T-228` has nothing to guess.

`T-228` — now claimed — serializes adapter, index and UI coexistence and inherits that locator
finding; finding 4 above is the one it starts from, and *What claiming `T-228` measured* says
what reading the tree added to it. `T-229` is the phase gate and runs alone.

**The Phase 2.2 gate is not met and must not be reported as met.** One measured no-payment path
now produces canonical captures *and* canonical runs — which is one gate clause of six, and the
runs that exist are committed test fixtures. Nothing has been ingested into `output/`, no
Twitter run reaches the library, the Reader or the Map, no Twitter command but `capture` exists,
and `WORKFLOW.md` still describes only the implemented YouTube workflow, which remains correct. `T-229` owns the
documentation and the failure rehearsal; do not update `WORKFLOW.md` before it.

**One gate clause still needs rewording rather than re-measuring.** The gate says "at least one
approved no-payment path works from the user's **actual Iran environment**". D-209 settles what
that environment is — Iran, through an always-on tunnel — so the clause should say so, and
`T-229` should verify the path through that tunnel rather than an egress the user never uses.
`T-224`'s live verification was run that way, and its captures record `via_tunnel: true` as a
stated fact (D-215).
